     pattern of one fault (eg missing judge names; second-hand quotations cited as originals; two or more over-citations);
     two or more slide citations or meta references, in any combination (R-Z(5)). Fair: citations do not support their
     propositions in more than one place. Poor: sources unacknowledged or cited to non-course material; more than
     three official-only footnotes. (v12: v11’s Poor for ‘a second Trident footnote’ is withdrawn with R-N: R-Y(1).)
     Marker comments. ‘Ensure your citation precisely supports the proposition for which it is relied.’ ‘The reference
     should not include [pages] that do not support the point.’ ‘case name should always be italicised (including in text).’

R9. Word count and format (1.0)

     Excellent: ‘Word count: N’ stated, N being the total of all text excluding footnotes as Word will count it, which is the
     verifier’s body count (headings included) plus the three words of the count line itself (R-P; CRIT_round3 M13,
     adopted: a body of 1,494 is stated as ‘Word count: 1,497’, and a stated count three words short of Word’s figure is a
     wrong count); N at most 1,500, so the body at most 1,497, and the sheet’s floors use 1,495 as the body ceiling;
     typed, double spaced, 12 pt Times New Roman, Arial or Calibri; page numbers; margins; MS Word; no coversheet,
     ‘unnecessary’ title page, headers or footers (page numbers excepted, the Instructions requiring them and forbidding
     only ‘unnecessary’ headers or footers: R-R(8); CRIT_round4 m16), bibliography, bullets or numbered points; no
     extended quotations. Any breach makes this row Poor (count wrong) or Omitted (over the limit or no count), with
     the Part 7 deductions.

R10. Consistency across Parts (1.0)

     Excellent: every consistency trap in Appendix A that the answer’s positions engage is either avoided or reconciled
     on one doctrinal footing, the six required ones (T4, T8, T10, T11, T13, T17) stated once in a clause where they bite
     and the rest avoided without words; narrator statements handled on the one R-M line throughout; the II.F2
     conclusion carried consistently into II.C2, II.D, II.I1, II.I3 and II.I4. VG: one tension unremarked. Good: two. Fair: a
     position in one Part undercuts another claim. Poor: the answer argues contradictory positions to win each claim.

Scoring rule

For each unit and each global row: points × multiplier (Excellent 1.00; Very Good 0.75; Good 0.60; Fair 0.45; Poor 0.20;
Omitted 0). Mark out of 100 = sum of all items − automatic deductions, never below 0, rounded down to a whole mark.
100 is awarded only if every unit and every global row is Excellent (each with the quoted sentence that earns it
and the source check) and no automatic deduction applies.

Calibration (R-C). His real LAWS1061 Part A grades, re-read cell by cell from the .pdf pages (v5; the recount stands,
was re-run for v6 and v8, and was confirmed by the round-7 critic and, from the rendered pages, by the round-8 critic; the
arithmetic is recomputed for v10): Preliminary Exc; 1(a) 3 Exc, 2 VG, 1 Good; 1(b) 1 Exc, 5 VG; 1(c) 2 Exc, 1 Good; 1(d) 2
Exc, 1 VG; 2 (statutory authorities) 2 Exc, 3 VG, 1 Good: 11 Excellent, 11 Very Good, 3 Good, no Fair, no Poor (25 cells);
the three global rows ungraded; Part A mark 82%. On these multipliers, with the printed Part weights (15/25/15/15/30,
the preliminary row unweighted) and equal weight within each Part, his dot grades give 0.850 × 15 + 0.792 × 25 + 0.867
× 15 + 0.917 × 15 + 0.808 × 30 = 83.5% of the dot points (v3 printed 83.7; the recount is 83.5); with the global block (20
of 100) capped at the grade of the units it summarises (Very Good on his sheet), the whole-sheet mark is 0.835 × 80 + 20
× 0.75 = 81.8, and 81.4 if R2 is capped at Good (his two ‘conclusive’ units); both within one mark of 82. The disclosure
the result depends on (CRIT_round4 m15, adopted): the dot points alone give 83.5, which is 1.5 off, and the within-one-
mark result holds only on the assumption that every ungraded global row is graded Very Good, the grade of the units it
summarises; if the five independent rows (R1, R3, R5, R7 and R9, 9.5 points) were Excellent, as his marker’s Part B style
comment might suggest, with R2 Good and the rest Very Good, the figure is 83.76 (v9 printed ‘83.7’, a truncation beside
the rounded 81.4: CRIT_round9 m6; R-U(6)). The assumption is a judgment call and is stated as the one on which the
calibration rests. The multipliers and the cap are unchanged from v2 and the grid change (23 units) does not touch the
calibration, which is run on his sheet’s own weights. The multipliers are a disclosed judgment call; the global-row cap is
what makes them reproduce his result.

Automatic deductions (applied after the grades; each instance counts)

One error, one deduction (R-U(6); CRIT_round9 m3 to m5). Each error is graded once: where the same fault
appears under two Drops lines or two automatic deductions, only the heaviest applicable applies (eg ‘ensure … promptly’
attributed to Rocky is D4 and not also D2; Knox CJ’s severance words as the joint judgment’s own are D2 at I.A and not
also a VG credit-wrong line; ‘[1980] AC 615’ is VG with D11 at II.I3 and not also Poor). The global-row cap (R-C) is not a
deduction and still applies, because it is what makes the global rows compound unit failures rather than offset them; D6’s
‘−2 each, in addition to the Poor grade’ is that deduction’s own rule and is unchanged. One error carried into a later
unit is deducted once, at the unit where it is made (R-V(1); CRIT_round10 m9): a later unit that applies the earlier
finding is graded on its own question and drops only for a new error of its own (eg ‘AMPL clearly gave none’ at II.I3 is
graded there, and ‘AMPL has nothing to enforce’ at II.I4, its consequence, is not deducted again; but a later unit that
turns the carried finding into new advice that the strength does not support, such as II.I4’s ‘so do nothing’ or ‘so pay now’
on an overstated date from II.G, makes a new error in the advice and is graded at that unit, R-U(4)).

     D1. Textbook or Optional file cited, quoted or relied on for any proposition, including as a page locator (R-Y(3)):
     −10 each, and the unit it supports is Poor. Lint (R-A): grep -nE "Principles of Contract Law" prints nothing, and any
     ‘Paterson’ or ‘Robertson’ hit is the casebook cited as a last resort under R-Z(3) in the R-A(4) form, at most twice.
     D2. Misattributed or misquoted authority: words attributed to the wrong case, judge, court or speaker (eg Lord
     Westbury’s or Jessel MR’s words as the Court’s own; Knox CJ’s severance principle as Taylor, Menzies and Owen JJ’s
     own; Megaw J’s ‘the counter-offer kills the original offer’ as Lord Denning MR’s coinage; Lord Greene MR’s words as
     said of the meetings example; Placer quoted as if from its extract; Sundell attributed to a named judge; the slide’s
     ‘we promise to fly you unless we choose not to’ attributed to Barwick CJ; ‘ensure … promptly’ attributed to Rocky is
     graded under D4, a speaker misidentified, and not here: CRIT_round9 m3); words in quotation marks not in the
     source as quoted; an ellipsis that changes meaning (eg Asquith LJ cut to ‘an agreement to forbear … is a good
     consideration’; Gibbs J spliced to ‘further terms to be settled by one of the parties … there would be no binding
     contract in such a case’; Winn v Bull without ‘merely’ quoted as the official text): −3 each, and the unit is Poor.
     D3. Wrong status on a point that matters: obiter as ratio (Godecke 646 to 647; Mahoney JA 176); dissent or
     minority as majority (Windeyer J and Barwick CJ in Coulls; McHugh JA in Beaton; Gibbs CJ at 581 stated as the
     Court’s view against Mason J (591) and Wilson J (598); Murphy J in Meehan); first instance as appellate (Banque
     Brussels; Todd v Nicol; Musumeci); a judge’s own route as the Court’s (Heydon JA at 179, 181, 182; Kirby P on
     consideration in Beaton); slide-only as casebook (Delaney); Mason J’s reasonable-efforts implication at 591 stated as
     a holding without its hedge: −1 each, and the unit is capped at Good. Skip-range status is never required and never
     deducted (R-F).
     D4. A fact not in the problem stated as fact, a derived fact not marked ‘arguably’ (eg ‘Grace never protested’;
     ‘Pierre did not know of the email’; ‘Rocky read the clause’; ‘AMPL drafted the Loan Agreement’; the Loan Agreement
     ‘purports expressly’ to be between AMPL and Grace; the clause ‘states’ or ‘records’ that Rocky guaranteed (the
     narrator describes its effect; its words are not narrated); the payments were made ‘on the 8th’; the payments ‘ended’
     or ‘stopped’ on 8 February 2025 (R-M proves payment in the period and no more: UPGRADES_r1 U16); the
     guarantee was agreed before the $10,000; the clause was written in after Pierre agreed; Crossley v Maycock’s facts),
     a speaker misidentified (the narrator’s ‘confirming the details’, ‘ensure … promptly’ or ‘personal guarantee provided
     by Rocky’ attributed to a party), or words in quotation marks that are not the problem’s words verbatim (dropped
     words without an ellipsis; altered words without square brackets; the setter’s own brackets in ‘when and how
     [Grace] intend[ed]’ not preserved; ‘$1 million dollar’ silently corrected): −2 each, and the unit is capped at Fair.
     D5. Conclusion before analysis at any level, including the answer’s first line: −1 each (R3 capped at Fair after
     two). A first sentence that classifies a quoted fact into the category whose test is then run is the issue, not a
     conclusion; an L-tier sentence in which the quoted fact and the rule’s operative words precede the result is not a
     conclusion first; a colon-joined sentence whose claim is a classification or a fact claim proved after the colon (‘The
     words are arguably ambiguous: …’; ‘Meehan is distinguishable: …’) is his form and is not conclusion-first; a unit’s
     VERDICT before a colon (‘Rocky lent nothing: …’) is conclusion-first whatever follows it (§1; R-R(2); CRIT_round4
     M8, adopted, narrowing CRIT_round3 m7). A first sentence that decides a contested element (‘Bullet 1 is an offer’;
     ‘Rocky is bound’; ‘No route around the rule is open’) before its analysis is D5.
     D6. Confidently wrong conclusion on a strong point (the advice would mislead the client): −2 each, in addition
     to the Poor grade. The list: Rocky can enforce the Loan Agreement against Grace; Rocky recovers under the 12
     February email; the clause in the Loan Agreement binds Rocky by itself; Rocky is not bound to anyone because he
     never signed; the $10,000 is payable now; there is no intention because Rocky and Pierre are cousins (presumption
     applied); a course rule fixes the start of the 12 months (from the date of the instrument or from drawdown); ‘ensure’
     or ‘once Grace repays’ held void for uncertainty.
     D7. Word limit. Body over 1,500 words including the count line: −5, R9 Omitted, and nothing after the 1,500th
     word is marked (this sheet’s rule; the UNSW Law & Justice penalty is applied separately under the Procedure, which
     is not in the course files). Count missing, or not equal to the verifier’s body count plus three (R-P: the line’s own
     three words are counted by Word): −2 (R-R(6); CRIT_round4 M13, adopted: the verifier’s own lint, ‘Stated word
     count N does not match the count N−3’, is the expected R-P consequence and is disclosed in the appendix, not a
     fault).
     D8. Format. Bullet or numbered points in the body; an extended quotation (any single quotation over 20 words, his
     longest in Part A being 19); quoted words over 20% of the body (R-G); a coversheet, an unnecessary title page,
     unnecessary headers or footers (page numbers excepted: R-R(8)), a bibliography: −1 each.
     D9. Voice mechanics. Em dash or en dash as punctuation, double quotation marks, contraction, first person: −0.5
     each, to a maximum of −3. A dash or contraction reproduced inside a quotation counts (this is Yusuf’s own rule, not
     his marker’s, who would not deduct for quoting the problem verbatim: CRIT_round3 m16, disclosed) (the problem
     itself contains both: ‘Bowral Project – wait and see’; ‘the Loan – the $1.15m’; ‘you can’t enforce anything’; ‘you won’t
     see a cent’; ‘if you don’t leave off’): quote around it, or quote only the words on one side of it. An en dash in a
     footnote pin range (eg 629–30) is correct AGLC and is not a fault. A quotation that itself contains a quotation is never
     reproduced with the inner quotation: quote only the outer words that carry the point, or paraphrase; an inner
     quotation reproduced is −0.5 under this item.
     D10. Italics. A case name or statute title not italicised (text or footnote): −0.5 each, to a maximum of −2.
     D11. Pao On cited other than [1980] AC 614 with pins 629–30 or 632, or with a wrong judge named (any name
     other than Lord Scarman for the Board); or a Pao On pin other than 629–30 or 632 given for the Lampleigh or Re
       Casey’s Patents passages: −1 each. ‘(Lord Scarman for the Board)’ is not a fault (R-P). A pin to Hobart 106 or 1 Ch
       115–16 inside an AGLC ‘quoted in’ form is the course extract’s own and is not a fault (CRIT_fidelity_2 m10, adopted).
       D12. Proxy terminology beyond R7’s cap: −0.5 each, to a maximum of −2.
       D13. Official texts and Trident. A fourth footnote resting on a proposition the course never states; a Trident CLR
       pin (none can be verified, the reading carrying no page markers: R-Y(1)); an official quotation not checked verbatim
       against the saved file: −1 each, and R8 capped at Good. (v12: v11’s ‘a second Trident footnote (R-N)’ is withdrawn,
       R-N being superseded; a Trident quotation is checked against the course reading as any course case is.)



Part 8. The v11 core costs (R-X(3)); the v10 floors kept as a record

What changed in v11. Until v10 this Part printed a floor table: each floor was the count of a real paragraph in his voice
carrying exactly the unit’s core (the paragraphs in SHEET_v10_appendix_NOT_FOR_DRAFTERS.md, 1,442 words with 52 heading words,
1,494 of 1,495, slack 1, verified with verify.py and voice_stats_v2.py), and that table was the proof that the Excellent standard
was attainable in 1,495 words (R-L(2) and (4)). The v11 cores, adopted from UPGRADES_r1 and R-X, cost more than that
table holds, so the floors no longer fit and are no longer the proof. Under R-X(3) the cores are the requirement and the
floors are a feasibility record, not a quota: the answer, not the appendix, is now the feasibility proof. The answer
reviser funds the v11 cores in R-X(3)‘s order ((a) zero-cost rewrites; (b) cutting duplication, cross-references,
restatements and credit text; (c) compressing units whose result is not in doubt: I.F1, P, I.F3, II.E, II.F1), never dropping a
unit’s core, and records anything still unfunded in the map as a residual risk graded by the unit’s Drops. No appendix
paragraph has been written or counted for v11; the costs below are the UPGRADES_r1 deltas (verify.py’s rule: whitespace
tokens containing a letter or digit) against the v10 counted forms, and the rulings’ own deltas, and they measure what the
v11 core adds, not the length a drafter must write.

Totals. v10 floors 1,442 + 52 = 1,494. v11 additions: UPGRADES_r1 net +76 (gross +83, less the tier-0 savings of U7 at
I.A, −3, and U3 at I.D and II.C2, −2 and −2), R-X(4)‘s ’the duty to co-operate’ +2, R-X(7)‘s deleted ’Frustration,’ −1: net
+77. v11 core cost 1,519 + 52 = 1,571 words at the v10 counted forms, 76 over the 1,495 ceiling (1,577 where a
draft also needs U24’s reason clause at II.G, and about 1,576 if condition precedent is named in II.H’s text rather than its
heading). The 76 words are found by the answer reviser under R-X(3), never by relaxing a core. UPGRADES_r1 section 2
ranks the paid items by marks at risk per word (rank 1, U16, 2.38 per word; rank 16, U7’s remainder, 0.04 to 0.06) for
that reviser’s use. Adding clauses raises the mean sentence length (his ceiling 24.7), so the reviser funds by cutting words
inside sentences and splitting long ones, and the R1 voice test is run on the answer itself (R-L(4) as R-X(3) now applies it).

v12 (R-Y; R-Z). Two core changes alter the counted cost, each a withdrawal, none an addition: P loses v11’s main clause
‘Breach, damages and guarantee law lie outside Weeks 1 to 4,’ (11 words) and its absolute ‘Grace having promised’
becomes ‘Grace promised’ (−1), so P’s v12 core form ‘Grace promised Rocky no reimbursement for paying AMPL’ plus the
Development Agreement clause costs about 24 (−12 against 36); II.I4 loses ‘which only postpones liability’ (−4, R-Y(4)),
about 71. Citation changes (Trident for the burden aspect and the routes; Port Jackson 240 or [11.70] for the agency
criterion; no slide) are footnote words and cost no body words. The v12 core cost is therefore about 1,555 at the v10
counted forms (1,519 − 16 + 52), still about 60 over the 1,495 ceiling, found by the answer reviser under R-X(3) as
before. The v11 table above is kept as the record it is.

What this means for the standard. The Excellent standard is the v11 cores, unchanged by length: (i) every unit’s core
is graded whatever the unit’s length (§1); (ii) every credit item is optional; (iii) the decide-once rules are applied; (iv) the L
units remain one or two sentences carrying the rule’s operative words; (v) the verdict sentences carry the strength, the
reason and the trailing further-information clause with its direction. If the answer reviser shows that a core cannot be met
within 1,495 words in his voice, the remedy is a disclosed demotion under the first-demotion list below, graded as printed,
or a ruling; never a silent relaxation. v4’s merge order is deleted (R-R(5); CRIT_round4 M12, adopted): each of its four
merges shrank an item its unit lists as core without saying whether the merged form still earned Excellent, which is the
silent relaxation R-B forbids. The ledgers below record how the v10 floors were reached; they are history, kept because
the first-demotion list and Part 9 refer to them.

reason (‘seeking a return promise’), VG. II.B: ‘nothing reserved a document or withdrawal’ (core (2), second limb), VG.

Loan’‘), VG. II.D: the Banque Brussels direction clause (’a mark of seriousness weaker here’), VG. II.E: the second
sentence (AMPL’s promise not illusory), VG. II.F1: the written-term reading (‘and even a written $10,000 term leaves him
the oral promise’), Good (one reading only, as the Drops say). II.F2: the consent clause (core (2), S2’s first clause, ‘AMPL
being ’owned and controlled’ by Pierre supplies consent’), VG (CRIT_round9 m7: consent has been a clause of S2 since
v6). II.G: the further-information tail (‘the agreement’s words would decide it’), VG. II.H: the duty to co-operate put and
met (S3 and S4), VG. II.I1: ‘signature being neither necessary nor decisive’ (T10), VG (R10 then VG as well). II.I2: ‘the
personal guarantee provided by Rocky’ tested, VG. II.I3: the fallback clause, VG. II.I4: the March instalment clause (core
(3)), VG. A drafter who cuts a different item is graded by the unit’s Drops, not by this list.

Items funded into core in v4 (the round-3 corrections; carried). I.A: the direction of Gibson 298 and the qualifier
carried to D and E in terms. I.C: AMPL’s price. I.E: Gibbs J’s 646 to 647 dictum with both hedges. I.F2: the II.G cross-

Part 9. Judgment calls in this sheet (disclosed)

  1. Weights carry v2’s (ISSUES_SETTLED S-28 re-balanced per CRIT_harsh F16), each merged unit taking the sum of
     the units it absorbs; the 80/20 split and the global-row weights are mine; the 12 months’ 5.0 now sits inside Part II
     (R-Q), so Part II is 51 and Part I 27.
  2. Multipliers (1.00, 0.75, 0.60, 0.45, 0.20, 0) and the global-row cap are mine; calibrated to his real result in Part 7
     (recounted cell by cell in v5, confirmed from the rendered pages by CRIT_round8 Lens 4, the arithmetic recomputed
     for v9 and v10: 83.5% of the dot points; 81.8 and 81.4 whole-sheet).
  3. Strength caps follow R-E: strong, contrary Poor; moderate to strong, contrary Very Good; moderate or weaker or
     open, either conclusion Excellent if best-reasoned; misstated strength Very Good, or Good where it changes the
     advice, symmetric both ways; no lean and no method at any open unit, Good (§2).
  4. The condition (II.H) is moderate to strong against Rocky under R-O (restoring S-16), v2’s ‘moderate’ being
     withdrawn: the fact of the events as described, that AMPL’s call does not itself prevent Grace from repaying, is a
     fact, not guarantee law, and answers Rocky’s co-operation and prevention arguments; since v5 it is the unit’s
     decisive reason in the counted paragraph and in core (4), now written in his ‘nothing suggests’ form and meeting the
     duty in its own words (‘omitted nothing necessary’), Mobil’s prevention analogy being credit from v6 (R-S(4)); the
     pricing point (‘no reward for the failure he undertook to prevent’) being, from v8, core (2)‘s answer to the
     commercial reading of Rocky’s words (CRIT_round7 M1; R-T(1)), while remaining no answer to the co-operation
     argument in its call form (that AMPL’s call stops Grace repaying), which core (4) meets on its own terms
     (CRIT_round4 M9; R-O); the stronger form of that argument (that AMPL, by accepting Rocky’s payment, would end
     any possibility that ’Grace repays the Loan’) is met in substance by the same pricing point, because a duty ‘to do
     everything necessary on their part to enable the plaintiff to have the benefit of the contract’ (CB2A 109, 185) enables
     the benefit S2’s construction defines, a reward priced on Grace’s repayment, and does not require AMPL to forgo
     payment under the undertaking to preserve it; the discharge question (what Rocky’s payment does to Grace’s debt)
     is guarantee law, not argued, never relied on and, from v12, never flagged as outside any scope (R-Z(1), (4);
     CRIT_round8 m6, recorded at II.H Core (4) at no cost); Lindley LJ’s ‘It is for the defendants to shew what it does
     mean’ remains no rule of onus (CB1A 44 to 45, read in full context). The duty to co-operate is cited as Mason J’s
     principle applied by McHugh JA (CB2A 109, 185), with no status word in the text (R-F), because McHugh JA’s
     minority on the result does not change the weight a court gives Mason J’s principle.
  5. The 12 February intention (I.D) is moderate on the course materials; the sheet allows either conclusion to reach
     Excellent and never requires the official-only passage (R-L(5)). Crossley’s facts are a gap and are never
     characterised (Z9). v3’s cap on an answer that deployed Crossley and concluded ‘bound’ is withdrawn (R-L(3)).
  6. II.D’s AMPL alternative is moderate to strong, not ISSUES_SETTLED’s strong (S-33; header C6): the reason is
     stated at the unit from the source page as R-R(9) requires (CRIT_round4 m12): Ermogenous [25] and [26] make the
     point an objective weighing of ‘the status of the parties to it, their relationship to one another, and other surrounding
     circumstances’ with the onus on the party alleging the contract, the casebook’s own note at [5.90] keeps relationship
     and context as ‘important considerations’, and Banque Brussels 523 is a first-instance judgment about a commercial
     letter between companies; no High Court authority in the materials decides a family-and-company weighing on these
     facts, so a contrary conclusion that applies [25] and [26] is a reasoned application of the governing authority, not law
     wrong, and is capped at Very Good (CRIT_complete_bias_2 B-4); a contrary conclusion resting on a family
     presumption remains Poor and D6.
  7. Citation of editors’ notes, official texts and Trident (v12: R-Y and R-Z; R-J, R-N, R-A(3) and R-P’s slide
     limb superseded). Trident is a Class 4A reading (R-Y(1)), so it is cited and quoted as a course case, one source per
     marker, with no cap of one footnote and no footnote counted as official-only toward R-H’s cap of three; the reading
     carries no CLR page markers, so it is cited by report with the judge named and no pin, and the verifier’s ‘report
     citation with no pinpoint’ lint is an accepted, disclosed consequence, of which a marker is told here. The burden
    aspect of privity is cited to Deane J (R-Y(2)), whose opening sentence states ‘the general rule that only the parties to
    a contract are bound by, and entitled to enforce, its terms’ (verified in the corpus’s Deane J segment). The routes
    around privity are cited to the Trident judge who states them, verified in the reading: Brennan J (‘It was not
    suggested that agency, trust or estoppel availed McNiece’; trust and agency each a ‘so-called exception’) or Mason
    CJ and Wilson J (trust and agency: ‘in the absence of a trust, an agency relationship or an enforceable agreement
    between the promisee and the third party’). The agency criterion is cited to a case first (R-Z(3)): Port Jackson 240
    (Barwick CJ, Lord Reid’s requisites (2) and (3), OFF Port Jackson [18] ‘(at p240)’), with Trident (Brennan J or Deane
    J) for the bare route and The New York Star for authority only; the casebook [11.70] remains accepted, at most
    twice, as the last resort for a sentence in the editors’ own ‘purporting … not solely’ form, which no case states in
    those words, and a reviser who can write the criterion in Lord Reid’s form uses Port Jackson instead. No slide is
    cited anywhere (R-Z(2)). Residual risk, disclosed (CRIT_round7 m6, carried): a real marker may record a report
    citation with no pinpoint as a minor non-compliance at R8; the slide form that once removed that risk is barred, so
    the risk stands, and the sheet grades the no-pin form as no fault. History: v4 to v11 allowed one Trident footnote (R-
    N), spent at II.I1, with ‘Class 4A Slides, slide 2’ as the accepted alternative; both rules are withdrawn.
 8. Slide citation form (withdrawn in v12: R-Z(2)). From v12 no slide is cited in the answer in any form, and R-A(3)
    and R-P’s slide limb are withdrawn, so the deck-and-slide form v4 to v11 prescribed (‘Class 4A Slides, slide 16’), its
    treatment as the disclosure under R-F, and the rejection of CRIT_round3 M6’s AGLC r 7.3 lecture form are all moot.
    A slide citation of any form is an R1 and R8 fault under R-Z(5) (VG for one instance, Good for two or more). The
    slides remain this sheet’s locators for the marker (the 4A deck’s header, ‘Term 3 2024 – JURD7150 | LAWS1150’
    against the file’s 2026, stays logged here); the reading guide names Professor Amelia Thorpe for the 4A lecture only.
 9. Extended quotation threshold (20 words) and the 20% aggregate are R-G.
10. Word-limit rule (nothing after the 1,500th word is marked) is this sheet’s rule. The ‘Word count: N’ line states
    Word’s figure for all text excluding footnotes, which includes the line’s own three words (R-P).
11. The casebook’s author order (reversed in v11: R-X(1); UPGRADES_r1 U14). S1A PDF page 7 (printed slide 8),
    the course’s own full citation, prints ‘Andrew Robertson and Jeannie Paterson, Contract: Cases and Materials
    (Lawbook Co, 15th ed, 2025) (known as the Contracts Casebook)’; the publisher’s listings (OUTSIDE, fetched by
    UPGRADES_r1) print ‘By Andrew Robertson, Jeannie Paterson’; reading guide 1A gives surnames only, in an
    informal definition (‘Contracts Casebook means Paterson and Robertson, …’). AGLC takes authors as the book
    presents them, and the reading guide’s surname order yields to the full citation. The sheet therefore prints ‘Andrew
    Robertson and Jeannie Paterson, Contract: Cases and Materials (Lawbook Co, 15th ed, 2025) [11.70]’, then
    ‘Robertson and Paterson (n X) [11.70]’. The title page is not in the folder, so the order remains a disclosed judgment
    call. v1 to v10 followed the reading guide (CRIT_fidelity_2 m9); that choice is withdrawn. The textbook lint (grep -nE
    "Principles of Contract Law") is unaffected.

12. ‘Ipp AJA’ per R-K; ‘Ipp JA’, the casebook’s own heading, is accepted and not deducted (CRIT_harsh_2 F17), because
    the marker reads against the casebook. ‘Rogers CJ’ as the casebook prints him; ‘Comm D’ is outside knowledge and
    is not used (CRIT_fidelity_3 m3).
13. The grid merges (Part 8) are the remedy R-B and R-L prescribe; they are disclosed there with every item moved

14. D5 on the first line is this sheet’s rule, stricter than his LAWS1061 marker, who graded his conclusive one-line
    preliminary Excellent (CRIT_harsh_2 F21); the standard may only rise. The colon carve-out (§1, D5) is confined
    since v5 to a classification or fact claim proved after the colon (R-R(2); CRIT_round4 M8): v4 had licensed a unit’s
    verdict before a colon on the strength of VOICE.md 1.3, overlooking VOICE.md section 4 item 10, which lists exactly
    that shape as a feature not to copy; I.F1’s counted sentence is rewritten in the L order at no cost. D9 on dashes and
    contractions inside quotations is Yusuf’s rule, not his marker’s (CRIT_round3 m16).
15. Nested quotations. The sheet does not state AGLC’s own nesting rule (not in the course files); it requires that no
    quotation containing a quotation be reproduced, because single-within-single is wrong and double marks are banned
    in the body.
16. The appendix’s conclusions on open points (not bound on 12 February; illusory; 27 February; AMPL the promisor;
    consideration given; Rocky assented; the condition not met by his own payment) were chosen only to make the
    counting real; the sheet grades the opposite conclusions as Excellent where best-reasoned, with the conditional
    cores stated at each unit.
17. II.F1’s ‘either reading Excellent’ departs from R-E as applied to S-10 (‘Reading A, moderate to strong’): the
    departure is deliberate, because the ambiguity in ‘the clause’ is textual and the legal result for Rocky is unchanged
    on both readings, so the sheet rewards running both rather than picking one (CRIT_round3 m14, disclosed).
18. Sundell 327. One rule (CRIT_fidelity_3 M8; CRIT_round3 m3): the court’s words exist in the course only in the
    editors’ note, so quoted words take the ‘quoted in’ form and spend a casebook footnote; a paraphrase without
    quotation marks is cited by report alone. The counted paragraph paraphrases, and from v12 the paraphrase by
    report is the preferred form, a case then carrying the proposition and the casebook being a last resort (R-Z(3)). This
    is a judgment call: the casebook prints the report pin ‘(at 327)’, so a report citation for a paraphrase is precise and is
    not a claim to have read the original.
19. Pao On’s judge. Either the Board (no name) or ‘(Lord Scarman for the Board)’ (R-P); the brief’s Part 3 sentence (‘no
    judge is named in the footnote’) was a rule of the run, which R-P now relaxes; the course extract itself names him.
20. The swap at I.E. Gibbs J’s Godecke 646 to 647 dictum (obiter; ‘Perhaps’ for subsidiary matters) is core and the
    content of the joint-promisee argument at I.F2 is credit, on the critic’s own ranking (CRIT_round3 Part 3.4): I.F2’s
    result is strong on ‘entered into’ whichever way the joint-promisee point is put, whereas I.E’s result turns on the very
    passage that addresses terms reserved to one party.
21. The II.H merge and the I.D fold. The two condition questions are one unit; Rocky’s and Grace’s best cases at I.D
    are carried as the clauses of the verdict sentence. Both are disclosed count-driven choices under R-L(1) and (2);
    neither drops a core item from the paragraph.
22. ‘II.G’ and ‘G’; course-vocabulary headings (R-X(6)). From v11 headings G, H and I may take a course-
    vocabulary form at no extra words (‘H. Condition precedent’); a longer course form is accepted but its words come
    from the answer’s allocation (§5). Inside the answer the cross-reference is ‘(G)’ or ‘the 12 months (G)’ because the
    heading is in the same Part (R-Q); in this sheet the unit is ‘II.G’. The heading ‘I. AMPL’s call’ sits under ‘II. ROCKY v
    AMPL (ALTERNATIVELY PIERRE)’ as that Part’s ninth lettered heading, and a reader scanning headings may
    misread ‘I.’ as a Part numeral; R-Q binds and the lettering is continuous, so the risk is noted here and not changed
    (CRIT_round4 m20); a drafter may write ‘I. AMPL’s call of 9 March 2025’ (+4) to make the letter read as a letter.
23. I.D’s opposing argument (CRIT_round4 M6, adopted in part). The critic was right that a K unit’s counter must
    be ‘put at full strength with its authority, and met’ and that v4’s ‘despite ’Confirmed’ settling the rest’ was a bare
    concession. Rocky’s best case on the course materials is Niesmann as the Court reports it at Masters 361 (all
    essential terms agreed; the one further stipulation ‘a condition of the obligation to pay’, not ‘of agreement’). The
    count funds the authority and the shape of the argument (‘despite ’Confirmed’ settling the essentials, as in
    Niesmann’, +3, footnoted Masters 361), met by ‘satisfactory conditions’ reserving the loan’s own terms to Rocky,
    which is Winn v Bull’s ‘new agreement the terms of which are not expressed in detail’ and not Niesmann’s mere
    condition of performance. The critic’s fuller form (+13: ‘although ’Confirmed’ arguably accepted a loan conditioned
    only in performance, as in Niesmann, ‘satisfactory conditions’ named no object and reserved the loan’s own terms to
    Rocky’) could not be funded without demoting I.C’s whole second sentence, which R-R(7) forbids unless the Loan
    Agreement’s consideration is still disposed of in one line with its authority. Residual risk, disclosed as R-R(7)
    requires: a lecturer who wants the analogy in terms may grade I.D Very Good on the counted form; a drafter who
    finds the words uses Niesmann 361 in the fuller form, which is credit and never deducted. The I.D ‘bound’ branch
    counted in the appendix (section 7) carries the fuller analogy with ‘arguably’ inside the floor (122 from v8), because
    its verdict sentence is shorter; the counted ‘not bound’ branch cannot (item 36 records the second and third residual
    risks at this unit). v11: the counted ‘settling the essentials’ becomes ‘settling amount, term and rate’ (UPGRADES_r1
    U26, +2), because ‘all essential terms’ is the premise of Gibbs J’s ‘Perhaps’ exception that I.E resists (trap T19); the
    analogy attaches to what ‘Confirmed’ settled, Niesmann having found a binding agreement.
24. The II.H verdict and T13. The counted II.H verdict is ‘His payment therefore likely does not meet it’, naming what
    it decides; T13 (Rocky could pay $1,150,000 and lose the $10,000) is required once where it bites (Appendix A: II.H
    or II.I4) and the counted structure states it at II.I4, so its restatement at II.H is credit since v5 (a disclosed R-L(3)
    move; Part 8). A drafter who states it at II.H and not at II.I4 is graded the same.
25. The two core-to-credit moves of v5 (R-L(3); R-R(7)): I.C’s ‘not the past advance’ with Roscorla 237 (the one-line
    disposal of the Loan Agreement’s consideration survives with its authority, the price criterion; the past-consideration
    point is decided at II.I3 where it bites, with Roscorla required there; this is the partial form of the demotion R-R(7)
    conditions, and the condition is met); and II.H’s restatement of T13 (item 24). Both are named at their units and in
    Part 8; neither lowers a core silently. The ranking that chose them: I.C’s tail carries at most 0.5 of 2.0 and re-makes
    a point another unit decides; II.H’s tail duplicates II.I4’s clause; every other candidate was a core item a critic had
    asked to be strengthened.
26. The R-P lint line. verify.py prints ‘Stated word count 1497 does not match the count 1494’ for the counted model
    because R-P makes the stated count Word’s figure, the body plus the line’s own three words; the lint is the expected
    consequence and is disclosed in the appendix section 3 and under D7 (R-R(6)); it overrides the brief’s Part 9 gate 2
    (‘equals that figure’) to that extent, and verify.py’s lint is unchanged.
27. ‘Narrator’ in this sheet. The sheet keeps ‘narrator’, ‘narrated’ and ‘narrative’ as its own analytic labels in §3 and
    the descriptors, because R-D and R-M are framed in them; they never appear in a core requirement’s prescribed
    wording, in the counted model or in the answer (R-R(1); R7).
28. What counts as a material finding from round 6 (R-S(5)). The appendix is a feasibility proof, not the answer (R-
    L(2)). A finding against this sheet is MATERIAL only if it shows (a) an error of law, source, pin, attribution or status
    in the sheet; (b) a core requirement a UNSW marker would not demand, or a demand the marker would make that
    no core carries; (c) a grading rule that contradicts the brief, the Instructions, ISSUES_SETTLED or R-A to R-Z (the
    rulings now run to R-Z: CRIT_round10 m11; v12); or (d) that a unit’s core cannot be met within its floor in his voice.
    An improvement to the appendix’s prose that shows none of (a) to (d) is MINOR and is adopted at the reviser’s
    discretion. Later markers and critics apply this definition before labelling a finding.
29. P’s clause for claim C3 (v12: R-Z(1) and (5)). v11’s form, ‘Breach, damages and guarantee law lie outside Weeks
    1 to 4, Grace having promised Rocky no reimbursement for paying AMPL’, is withdrawn because its main clause
    names matters as ‘outside Weeks 1 to 4’, meta language R-Z(1) bars and R-Z(5) grades at R1 and R8. The v12
    accepted form is a substantive clause, ‘Grace promised Rocky no reimbursement for paying AMPL’ (eight words), or
    the absolute ‘Grace having promised Rocky no reimbursement for paying AMPL’ trailing a substantive main clause;
    ‘for paying AMPL’ stays (UPGRADES_r1 U31, so that a cold reader can name the claim), and ‘no reimbursement’
    stays in preference to CRIT_round5 M5’s ‘nothing’, because ‘nothing’ sits one Part above I.C’s inference that
    ‘Confirmed’ arguably implied Grace’s promise to repay and a marker could read the two as inconsistent; either is
    Excellent. History: v6 to v10 printed ‘Frustration, breach, damages and guarantee law lie outside Weeks 1 to 4,
    Grace having promised Rocky no reimbursement’ (+6 in v6; the clause moved after the main clause in v7 at no cost:
    CRIT_round6 m11); v11 deleted ‘Frustration,’ (R-X(7)) and added ‘for paying AMPL’. The answer may omit a
    preliminary paragraph (R-Z(1)); C3 and the Development Agreement are then graded at P wherever the answer
    disposes of them.
30. The AGLC form for a source quoting or citing another (CRIT_round5 m6; R-S(6); CRIT_round9 M4; R-
    U(5)). The AGLC text is not in the course files; it was read for this point only, in the MULR AGLC4 PDF
    (law.unimelb.edu.au, fetched on 11 October 2026 by CRIT_round9 and again, as a text extract, by the round-10
    reviser), and it is never cited in the answer. Rule 1.3, ‘Sources Referring to Other Sources’, has no sub-rules (v6 to
    v9 printed a sub-rule number that does not exist; corrected throughout in v10); it lists ‘quoting’, ‘citing’ and like
    phrases, which ‘should be preceded by a comma’, and its example, ‘Burger King Corporation v Hungry Jack’s Pty Ltd
    (2001) 69 NSWLR 558, 570 (Sheller, Beazley and Stein JJA), quoting …’, places ‘, quoting’ after the pinpoint and the
    judge’s bracket. The sheet adopts that form, followed by the quoted source’s citation as the casebook page prints it
    (Eccles v Bryant [1948] Ch 93, 104 from v11, the report as the Court in Masters prints it at CB3A 156 and the official
    Masters text prints it, CB1B 77’s ‘[1948] 1 Ch 93’ being Heydon JA’s own slip, logged and not followed: R-X(2);
    UPGRADES_r1 U8; CB1B 78 for Integrated Computer Services (1988) 5 BPR 11,110, 11,117–18; CB2A 107 for
    Thomas v Thomas (1842) 2 QB 851, 859; 114 ER 330, 333–4; CB2A 109 for Secured Income (1979) 144 CLR 596,
    607; CB3B 161 for G Scammell & Nephew Ltd v Ouston [1941] AC 251, no pin printed at 437; CB3A 156 for Winn v
    Bull (1877) 7 Ch D 29, 32, from v10 at fn 8), the judge of the quoted source in brackets. v5’s form (the quoting clause
    inside the judge bracket; the quoted source uncited) is withdrawn. v9’s hedge, that a marker who checks the AGLC
    and finds a different placement grades the form as one minor non-compliance, is withdrawn, the placement being
    verified. Rule 1.4.3, read at the same time, says that ‘ibid’ ‘should not be used where there are multiple sources in
    the preceding footnote’, so the footnote after a ‘, quoting’ or ‘, citing’ footnote takes the ‘Short title (n X) pin’ form
    (fn 9, ‘Masters (n 7) 361’, from v10; every other footnote after a two-source footnote in the counted model, fn 20, 22,
    25, 33 and 41, already does).
31. II.H’s literal reading. The counted S2, ‘Rocky’s payment, ’once Grace repays the Loan’ naming Grace, would not
    literally meet it’, states the literal reading of Rocky’s own words before the counter and leaves the verdict to S5 after
    it (R-S(2); CRIT_round5 M3). ‘Literally’ is the one-word form of the sheet’s own ‘on their face’ (core (2)); ‘on its face’
    (+1) is equally acceptable. A drafter who writes the unhedged ‘would not meet it’ or ‘objectively would not meet it’
    at that point states the verdict before the counter (VG; D5).
32. Weights (CRIT_round5 m13, noted). I.F2 carries 7.5 points on an 81-word C unit and II.C2 5.5 on a 126-word K
    unit. The asymmetry is deliberate: the weights follow ISSUES_SETTLED S-28’s ranking by marks at stake and are
    disclosed in Part 2, and R5 says privity against Grace is complete but brisk because authority decides it; the words a
    unit needs measure its difficulty, not its weight. No change.
33. Empirnall at II.I2 (CRIT_round5 m14, noted as a residual risk). McHugh JA’s criterion at 535 to 536 concerns
    an offeree who takes the benefit of goods or services offered on terms; the model’s benefit is a conditional promise of
    $10,000 that was the consideration for Rocky’s own offer. The sheet applies the criterion as the course’s nearest
    authority (settled in rounds 3 and 4) and keeps Rocky’s counter (Kirby P at 170, by analogy) and the ticket-case line
    (confined to carriage and bailment) in credit. A lecturer may regard the analogy as strained; a drafter who says so in
    a clause and still applies the criterion loses nothing.
34. Upper Hunter 437 and Ermogenous [25] at II.H (CRIT_round5 m5; CRIT_round6 m4; CRIT_round7 M1
    and m15, adopted; R-T(1)). The pin was corrected to 437 in v6. Read in context, Barwick CJ’s ‘no narrow or
    pedantic approach’ leans toward a commercial reading of Rocky’s words; v7 answered that by swapping fn 39 to
    Ermogenous [25], which hid the passage from the marker without answering it (CRIT_round7 M1). From v8 the
    counted S2 puts the commercial reading and meets it with the pricing point, so fn 39 is Upper Hunter 437 again,
    placed at the end of the sentence, the marker who checks it finding the passage the clause answers; Ermogenous
    [25] remains the accepted alternative; neither is deducted. The preference is stated: 437 where the sentence
    engages the commercial reading, as the counted model does; [25] is a neutral statement of the objective test and is
    equally correct.
35. The moves of v6 (R-S(4)). Mobil 500 at II.H to credit; the restatements at II.C2 S6 and II.G S3 removed; II.F2’s
    credit fact removed from the counted sentence; each named at its unit and in Part 8 with its words. I.F3 is not
    absorbed. No core item was lowered silently; the per-unit first-demotion list (Part 8) prints the grade that follows any
    further cut.
36. Ermogenous [25]‘s relationship factors at I.D, and the Masters categories (CRIT_round6 m12;
    CRIT_round7 m1; CRIT_round8 m9; residual risks recorded beside item 23, not changes).** The second
    risk (CRIT_round7 m1): the counted I.D paragraph never names a Masters category, reasoning through
    Winn v Bull at 363 instead, and a marker applying the reading guide’s ’different categories of preliminary
    agreement’ may ask which class the email falls in; ‘as the third class contemplates’ (+2) is credit a
    drafter writes where words allow, the slack of 1 not funding it, and the sheet does not require it because
    363, not the classes, is the decisive authority for a qualifier that mentions no formal contract. The first
    risk (CRIT_round6 m12): [25] lists ‘the status of the parties to it, their relationship to one another, and
    other surrounding circumstances’ (CB3A 137), and II.D weighs them (‘cousins’; ‘at AMPL’s office’); at I.D
    the counted paragraph weighs only the email’s words, ‘project developers’ and ‘since 2017’ sitting in
    credit. A marker applying [25] may expect one clause (‘between project developers’, +3), which the slack
    of 3 could fund only by spending every word of it; the sheet does not spend it, because item 23’s
    Niesmann form (+13) is the larger residual risk at the same unit and the two cannot both be funded.

46. I.F2 S1’s rule stated without the joint-promisee qualification (CRIT_round9 m12; a residual risk recorded

    a contract’ is the course’s rule; but Coulls 486 (Taylor and Owen JJ, CB4 330) lets a joint promisee recover
    ‘notwithstanding that she, personally, gave no consideration’, the course’s answer to RG4A’s multi-party question.
    The counted S2 meets the joint-promisee argument on its premise (no promise to both), so the result is right; a

    aside, can enforce a contract’ (+3) is not funded at slack 1.

Appendix A. Consistency traps (ISSUES_SETTLED 3.X, reduced to those a position
engages)

Required, one clause each where it bites (R10): T4 asserting the 12 February email invites Grace’s counterclaim (I.D
credit; engaged only if the answer holds the email binding); T8 class 3 or a writing requirement for the 8 April bargain
loses both sides (II.B or II.C2); T10 signature cuts both ways (I.F2 and II.I1); T11 one counterparty, or Pierre on both
sides (II.F2 and II.I4); T13 Rocky could pay $1,150,000 and lose the $10,000 (II.H or II.I4; the counted structure states it
at II.I4); T17 AMPL cannot have both 27 February arguments (II.C2 or II.I3; engaged only if the answer accepts AMPL’s
sequence and runs past consideration).

Avoided without words (no clause required; a breach is graded at R10): T1 the 12 months (demand v defence;
decided once at II.G); T2 party to the Loan Agreement v burden defence (reconciled, where words allow, by the four-word
credit clause ‘as to that clause only’ at II.I1: CRIT_round5 m12); T3 ‘I will do that’ binds both ways (carried by II.D’s
clause); T5 ‘the Loan Agreement with AMPL’ (the narrator’s description of the demand, usable as its proven content
under R-D, never as Rocky’s words) undercuts any claim that the money was Rocky’s; T6 class 3 or illusory defeats the
email claim but protects against the counterclaim; T7 past consideration for the undertaking v consideration for the
$10,000; T9 ‘once Grace repays’ read to include Rocky’s payment fights his own words; T12 attacking Grace’s
consideration destroys the $10,000 trigger; T14 check-ins go to formation, not meaning; T15 keep the 12 February email
and the Loan Agreement separate; T16 the two readings of ‘the clause’ (both run at II.F1); T18 the a fortiori reasoning
confined to the guarantee reading.

Avoided by the core’s own wording (from v11; a breach is graded once, at R10): T19 I.D’s ‘Confirmed’ settling ‘the
essentials’ (or ‘all essential terms’) concedes the premise of Gibbs J’s ‘Perhaps it may be different where agreement has
been reached on all essential terms but the determination of subsidiary matters has been left to one of the parties’ (CB3B
190, Godecke 647), which I.E core (3) then resists; avoided by naming what ‘Confirmed’ settled, ‘amount, term and rate’
(UPGRADES_r1 U26). T20 I.C finding consideration without qualification while I.E finds Rocky’s promise illusory stands
on two footings, ‘A contract made for a consideration which is illusory is unenforceable’ (CB3B 166, Pace 150); avoided by
I.C’s ‘in form’, whether the promise is illusory, and so the consideration good, being decided at E (UPGRADES_r1 U10).

Appendix B. Claims, and the planted facts that decide each unit (so R4 can be applied
from this sheet alone)

Claims (ISSUES_SETTLED 1.4, condensed). C1 repayment of the principal ‘under the Loan Agreement with AMPL’
against Grace (fails: privity); C2 repayment under the 12 February email (fails: no binding contract and Rocky lent
nothing); C3 any implied promise by Grace to repay, reimburse or indemnify (no promise by Grace to Rocky appears;
disposed of in a substantive clause in P or wherever the answer first meets it, never with guarantee law named as
excluded: R-Z(1)); C4 the Development Agreement (stipulated ‘legally binding’; Rocky’s entitlement contingent on a profit
‘not yet shown’; a non-issue to be named as one, not a claim, in a clause in P: CRIT_round7 m11; R4 grades its omission as
a non-issue unnamed, VG); C5 the Eastwood and Bowral items (not promissory; I.A); C6 the $10,000 against AMPL,
alternatively Pierre (formed; not yet payable; conditional); C7 the $10,000 as a clause of the Loan Agreement on the other
reading of ‘the clause’ (adds nothing; II.F1); C8 Rocky’s answer to AMPL’s 9 March 2025 call (II.H).

Deciding planted facts (mandatory, quoted) by unit (R-L(7)); each is quoted in the counted paragraph of its
unit. P: ‘legally binding’; ‘if the Project made a profit’; claim C3 identified and named in a substantive clause, ‘Grace
promised Rocky no reimbursement for paying AMPL’ (from v11: U31; from v12 never attached to a clause naming
excluded matters: R-Z(1); frustration’s planted fact, ‘unavoidable delays’, no longer deciding: R-X(7)) (no quoted fact: the
problem contains no promise by Grace to Rocky, ‘Confirmed’ being her only promissory word, and that at I.C:
CRIT_round5 M5). I.A: ‘proposed that Rocky offer’; ‘I will lend’; ‘Please confirm agreement’; ‘Potential’ and ‘wait and see’
(the bullets’ deciding words, from v11: U15); ‘subject to satisfactory conditions’ (deciding here too, since the similarity to
Lord Diplock’s second paragraph cannot be named without it: CRIT_round3 M1). I.B: ‘Confirmed’; ‘15 minutes later’. I.C:
‘at a rate of 5% interest’; ‘4% per annum’ (the counted form quotes ‘12 months’ as well, with ‘likely’ marking the
inference that the recorded term carries AMPL’s promise; ‘not the past advance’ is credit). I.D: ‘satisfactory conditions’
(or ‘subject to satisfactory conditions’); ‘Confirmed’ (or ‘Please confirm agreement’), now carried with its authority (‘as in
Niesmann’); ‘project developers’ (from v11, the Ermogenous [25] status factor: U4); ‘this morning’ (the further
information, with its direction: U3). I.E: ‘satisfactory terms and conditions’ is Meehan’s, not the problem’s; none of the
problem’s beyond the reading carried from D (credit: ‘satisfactory conditions’ quoted again). I.F1: ‘$1,150,000’; ‘$1
million’; ‘Rocky asked’ (met); the guarantee clause (from v11 core: U5). I.F2: ‘entered into’; ‘I never signed anything with
you’ (quoted or answered in terms); ‘5 March’ (the demand, for the cross-reference). I.F3: none beyond the facts named.
II.A: ‘expected’; ‘I will do that if you also promise’. II.B: ‘Pierre agreed’. II.C1: ‘if you also promise’. II.C2: ‘also’; ‘all
payments … promptly’; ‘repayment of the Loan’. II.D: ‘promise’; ‘to me directly’; ‘at AMPL’s office’; the cousinhood named
